### April 16, 2026 | News Release | Corporate Treasury | Geopolitical Risk Concerns Surge for Corporate Treasurers, According to 2026 Tradeweb ICD Portal Client Survey
https://www.tradeweb.com/newsroom/media-center/news-releases/geopolitical-risk-concerns-surge-for-corporate-treasurers-according-to-2026-tradeweb-icd-portal-client-survey/
The annual survey, now in its ninth year, was conducted in January 2026 and found that nearly nine in ten respondents (88%) reported moderate-to-high concern around the current geopolitical environment, with almost half (48%) citing “high concern” – an 11-percentage point increase from last year’s survey. Follow-on polling in early March 2026, conducted after the start of the conflict in the Middle East, showed that figure rising further from 48% to 68%.
Against this backdrop, treasurers are adopting a more defensive investment strategy. In 2026, 27% of firms plan to increase their MMF allocations, compared with just 9% planning to decrease them, reinforcing a broader “flight to safety” amid macro uncertainty. Cash balance expectations have become more balanced year-over-year – with 29% of respondents expecting increases and 23% expecting decreases, versus 31% and 18%, respectively, in 2025 – suggesting corporate treasurers are reallocating liquidity rather than building cash positions.
“We’re seeing a distinct change in how treasurers are managing liquidity amid heightened geopolitical uncertainty,” said Tom Britton, Head of International Corporate and Global Corporate Product at Tradeweb. “Rather than simply building cash positions, firms are becoming more deliberate in how they allocate it, with a growing focus on instruments that offer stability, transparency and immediate access. In this environment, electronic trading is playing an increasingly important role in helping firms access and manage liquidity more efficiently in real time.”
“These findings highlight a clear shift in how treasurers are approaching liquidity in an increasingly uncertain world,” said Daniel Staniford, Head of Americas Corporate and Global Corporate Sales at Tradeweb. “As geopolitical risk intensifies, treasury teams are prioritizing flexibility, speed and access to liquidity, driving growing interest in innovations such as tokenized money market funds and 24/7 markets. While adoption remains early, this marks a meaningful step toward a more digital, always-on liquidity ecosystem.”

### August 5, 2026 | In the News | Tradeweb | Interview with the CEO: Billy Hult, Tradeweb
https://www.tradeweb.com/newsroom/media-center/in-the-news/interview-with-the-ceo-billy-hult-tradeweb/
The original idea was that there were types of transactions that could be automated and done in a more efficient way over the internet. The concept of a salesperson standing on the trading floor and asking multiple dealers to provide liquidity to the client led someone to look at that transaction and say, “Isn’t there a better way to do this? Isn’t there a more valuable use of this person’s time than just being essentially a middleman on a standard transaction?”
Mr. Hult: We continue to evolve, I think, from the concept of expansion. We started off in government bonds but, in a really good way, we didn’t just want to be a one-asset-class company. We wanted to be a global company that could take what we learned in Treasuries and extend that across marketplaces.
Mr. Hult: We have a four-pronged and really amazing network. I think if I was going to go back in time, I would say one of the things the company did really well in the very beginning was to establish this very formidable and real network of clients.
Mr. Hult: I think we have an orientation to be able to keep our eye on our day jobs, which is continuing to be leaders and expanding in these traditional financial markets that we’re known for. At the same time, I think we have a very strong perspective that the frontier markets in our space are, without question, important and becoming more important.
Mr. Hult: I don’t know if it’s hesitancy. I would say we are eyes wide open, very well aware all the time. We have a very strong reputation. I think we have a reputation of being a very credible marketplace, and there’s a lot of noise in the space. We know that, and we see that, and we see the headlines around all of that.
That being said, I think it’s a smaller bet that we’re making. We still feel very strongly it’s an important bet, and sometimes you have to make the right bet on seeing that there will be changes in behavior and changes in outcome. We’re willing to do that. But as the expression goes, “Where do you go to get your reputation back? You don’t.”
We take our reputation as an industry leader and a trusted platform in our network of clients extremely seriously. Small doses around this fits us really well, and we’re comfortable with it. We’re moving forward with a partnership there, and we think it’s going to work out.
Mr. Hult: We’re always exceptionally oriented towards adding to this vaunted network that we have. All three of those deals added significant participants into our network.
We’ve largely gotten here as builders, not acquirers. A lot of the growth that we’ve had as a company has come through our own organic build and our own eyes around where we see opportunities for technology to be applied into these marketplaces. That being said, as a public company, we’re ambitious.
Going forward, I think the general view is that we’re going to have opportunities to continue to make the right kind of consolidation plays inside of our business, and we’re going to be very, very open to that. The core belief is that the growth that we have, the credibility that we have as a company making the right kind of acquisitions, is only going to lead us towards more potential outcomes. But we’re going to be rigorous first and foremost around companies that fit our eye.
Mr. Hult: We get approached all the time from amazingly interesting startup AI companies that come to us and say, “How we can help you get your development cycle going faster,” or things like that. We’ll be open-minded around that.
Mr. Hult: What’s that expression — “Nobody knows anything”? It’s hard to predict what’s going to happen. What we would say very strongly is we really try to be laser-focused on things that we can control.
We know we have a great business, we know we connect with our clients all the time, we know there’s a lot of wind at our sails around the continued electronification of the marketplaces that we live and breathe in, and we know that we’re the kind of company that likes to spend time with our clients on solving problems. I think if you focus on those things, you’re on the right path.
And the other stuff, it’s not just noise, because obviously it affects us, stock price affects us, and all of those things. We live in the real world. But ultimately, we try to separate out the things that we can control versus the things that just exist out there in the marketplace that are beyond our control. I think when you think like that, you put yourself in a pretty good position.
Mr. Hult: I think the headwinds for the sector are around the unknown of what technology brings to the equation. What you’re getting more and more is, do marketplaces change, and do they change in such dramatic ways that I can’t tell who will be the winners and the losers five years from now or 10 years from now?
But you focus on what you can control. From my perspective, when I think about the business that we have, I start with scale. We were over $2 billion in revenue last year, and we’re having a very good year this year. So the scale is real.
That being said, you never take anything for granted. So the concept of not taking your eye off the ball and maintaining an intensity around change, I think, is fundamentally important.
Mr. Hult: Meet the management team. Spend time with management teams. I think the more investors get to know management teams, I think that’s a really good starting point.
I think past records matter, but really at the end of the day, when you close your eyes and imagine what the world’s going to look like, imagine what kind of companies are going to navigate the future the best.

https://www.tradeweb.com/newsroom/media-center/in-the-news/first-fully-onchain-repo-transaction-completed-using-sovereign-digital-bond/
Jordan Goldman, President and COO, M1X Global: “Derivatives and secured financing markets have been waiting for collateral that works across institutional and digital rails simultaneously. USDM1 is a secured sovereign digital bond - not a stablecoin, not a tokenized fund, not a CBDC. It combines the legal framework and capital treatment institutional counterparties require with 24/7 settlement. This transaction demonstrates for the first time what onchain sovereign collateral looks like in production.”
Dan Eckstein, Head of Rates Sales, Virtu Financial: “Virtu's business is built on providing liquidity efficiently across every market we operate in. Capital efficiency is not an abstract concept for us - it shows up directly in our ability to deploy working capital and serve clients. USDM1 addresses collateral constraints that have limited onchain capital markets and prevented them from reaching institutional scale. We are committed to helping build the foundational infrastructure this market requires.”
Liz Kirby, Head of Market Structure, Tradeweb: “We see tokenization as an important next step in the evolution of electronic trading and market infrastructure. The completion of this transaction demonstrates how digitally native sovereign collateral and atomic settlement can enhance collateral capital efficiency and modernize repo workflows, all while maintaining the institutional standards market participants have come to expect. We are pleased to collaborate with other industry leaders on developing and advancing practical solutions that support the continued evolution of institutional markets.”

Mohammed Al Shaikh, Chief Treasury and Investment Officer at SAB, commented: “SAB was the first local market maker to execute a domestic KSA Sukuk trade on Tradeweb’s platform. Muqassa’s contribution to this initiative reflects its broader role in supporting the continued development, efficiency, and connectivity of Saudi Arabia’s debt capital market.”

### September 30, 2026 | In the News | Derivatives | Tradeweb Streamlines Bilateral Swap Unwinds
https://www.tradeweb.com/newsroom/media-center/in-the-news/tradeweb-streamlines-bilateral-swap-unwinds/
“This is going to be an important stepping stone in the evolution of the electronification of bilateral swaps,” says Todd Coletto, head of rates business development for the UK and Europe at Citi.
With tenors up to 50 years, many of these instruments are unwound prior to maturity – a “hugely complicated” process, according to Enrico Bruni, co-head of global markets at Tradeweb.
David Morris, BlackRock’s head of rates trading, EMEA, describes the approach as “straightforward and highly efficient”. Details were agreed electronically on the platform, with confirmation and settlement completed on MarkitWire “within one minute”.
“You have a big, sophisticated client who makes the first move, and it takes a little bit of time for the rest of the industry to catch up. I think that liability‑driven investment asset managers and GBP focused interest rate participants who have the same profile will come onboard relatively quickly,” he says.
“We’re very keen to pursue this market segment, which, for valid reasons, has resisted electronification. By focusing on these three components, one after the other, we should be able to bring the efficiency of the electronic world to the non-cleared space,” says Bruni.
“Electronic platforms like Tradeweb have introduced significant efficiencies into the market,” he says. “And I think the industry has gotten used to trading in a specific way. They ask: ‘If I can do this for cleared swaps, why can’t I do it for bilateral?’.”
“It’s following the same trajectory as cleared flow: the more vanilla, simpler trades are already going through the platform, and the scope is there to expand into the more complex pricing aspects.”
BlackRock’s Morris says services that reduce operational risk by eliminating manual processes and deliver time savings could make bilateral swap markets more attractive.
“Electronic workflows have the potential to make bilateral interest rate swap markets more accessible by improving visibility into available liquidity and making it easier for participants to engage with a broader range of counterparties,” says Morris.

### October 19, 2026 | News Release | Credit | Tradeweb to Acquire Trumid, Expanding Tradeweb’s Credit Capabilities Across the Entire Bond Lifecycle and Further Differentiating its Multi-Asset Class Platform
https://www.tradeweb.com/newsroom/media-center/news-releases/tradeweb-to-acquire-trumid-expanding-tradewebs-credit-capabilities-across-the-entire-bond-lifecycle-and-further-differentiating-its-multi-asset-class-platform/
NEW YORK – October 19, 2026 – Tradeweb Markets Inc. (Nasdaq: TW) (“Tradeweb” or the “Company”), a global leader in electronic trading facilitating more than $3.0 trillion in value per day across asset classes, announced that it has entered into a definitive agreement to acquire Trumid Holdings, LLC (“Trumid”), a financial technology company and electronic credit trading marketplace, for a purchase price of approximately $1.97 billion, subject to customary adjustments, which is comprised of $1.75 billion for the business and approximately $220 million[1] for tax assets being acquired.
Billy Hult, CEO of Tradeweb, said: “This acquisition represents an important step forward in our ambition to make credit markets more efficient and provide clients with smarter ways to access liquidity. We believe U.S. credit represents a significant long-term growth opportunity, particularly as the market continues to embrace electronic execution and as clients seek increasingly sophisticated solutions to navigate liquidity and achieve better outcomes. Trumid has built an innovative platform that is well positioned for where the market is headed. By bringing our capabilities together under Tradeweb, we can accelerate innovation, broaden the solutions we offer clients, and create an even more differentiated trading experience. We are excited about this combination and the value we can deliver together to clients over the long term.”
Mike Sobel, Co-CEO and President of Trumid, said: “We look forward to incorporating our innovative protocols, agile technology, and collaborative client-centric approach to product development into Tradeweb’s global footprint and client network. Our teams share dual passions for technology and client experience. Together, we’ll build on what we've created, accelerate innovation, and deliver an even stronger and more intelligent trading experience for our combined networks of buy and sell-side clients globally. I’m personally excited to continue the momentum as part of the Tradeweb team.”
